EXECUTIVE SUMMARY & ROOT CAUSE ANALYSIS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. PROMPT 1: "start the Sprint 2"
   - Start Time: 2026-09-04 12:59:56 UTC [18:29:56 IST] (Step 240)
   - End Time:   2026-09-04 13:39:41 UTC [19:09:41 IST] (Step 777)
   - Duration:   ~39 minutes 45 seconds
   - Goal: Implement Sprint 2 deliverables: benchmark_logger.py and uncoordinated_choke_test.py,
           then run the baseline benchmark test of 2 AMRs colliding at the choke point.
   - What was accomplished:
     * Created src/amr_simulation/scripts/benchmark_logger.py
     * Created src/amr_simulation/scripts/uncoordinated_choke_test.py
     * Updated src/amr_simulation/CMakeLists.txt to install scripts
     * Built packages with colcon
   - Why it worked for so long and failed / timed out:
     * FATAL Nav2 Controller Failure: Nav2 controller server failed to configure with:
       "Failed to create controller. Exception: According to the loaded plugin descriptions
        the class nav2_mppi_controller::MPPIController with base class type nav2_core::Controller
        does not exist. Declared types are nav2_regulated_pure_pursuit_controller::RegulatedPurePursuitController"
     * The nav2_params.yaml configured RegulatedPurePursuitController, but because the nodes were
       running under namespaces (/amr1, /amr2, /amr3), ROS 2 parameter remappings and include
       file parameters failed to match the fully qualified node names (/amr1/controller_server).
     * When parameters failed to match, Nav2 1.5.1 defaulted in C++ to MPPIController, which
       was not compiled/available in the system, crashing the controller server on startup.
     * The assistant spent ~38 minutes in repetitive diagnostic loops:
       - Modifying nav2_bringup.launch.py and multi_robot_warehouse.launch.py
       - Rebuilding workspace
       - Relaunching Gazebo and Nav2 stack (~8 times)
       - Waiting through long asynchronous sleep intervals (5s, 8s, 10s, 15s, 20s, 35s)
       - Checking action servers and finding them inactive
       - Repeating parameter file experiments without addressing the fully qualified names.

2. PROMPT 2: "Check the Sprint2, and what has been done already, it was working for 38 minutes, find out what took it this long then complete the sprint2"
   - Start Time: 2026-09-04 13:39:41 UTC [19:09:41 IST] (Step 778)
   - End Time:   2026-09-04 13:54:51 UTC [19:24:51 IST] (Step 922)
   - Duration:   ~15 minutes 10 seconds
   - Goal: Investigate why Prompt 1 was stuck for 38 minutes, fix it, and complete Sprint 2.
   - What was accomplished:
     * Diagnosed the root cause of MPPIController: inspected nav2_controller binary strings and
       parameter_handler.hpp defaults. Confirmed that Nav2 falls back to MPPIController if the node
       namespace does not match the YAML top-level keys.
     * Killed stale background tasks and simulation processes.
     * Rewrote nav2_bringup.launch.py with an OpaqueFunction to dynamically generate per-robot
       YAML files in /tmp with fully-qualified node names (e.g., /amr1/controller_server).
     * Successfully verified that controller_server created RegulatedPurePursuitController for all 3 AMRs.
   - Why it failed / stalled before completion:
     * Immediately after fixing the controller type, Nav2 hit a secondary fatal transform error:
       - "Could not find a connection between 'amr1/odom' and 'amr1/base_footprint'"
       - "Failed to activate local_costmap because transform from base_link to map did not become available"
     * The local costmap was defaulting to robot_base_frame = base_link (instead of amr1/base_footprint)
       and the TF bridge / DiffDrive plugin transforms published on /amr1/tf were not linked to the static transform.
     * While inspecting TF frames (Steps 907-909), the model API experienced consecutive overload errors
       (Steps 911, 913, 918, 920: "Error: The model API is currently overloaded"), stalling the execution.
       static transform map -> amr1/odom.
     * The assistant was actively inspecting TF topic frames and echo outputs when the prompt timed out
       without executing the final choke test.

[2026-09-04 13:42:29 UTC [19:12:29 IST]] [STEP 819] [AGENT ANALYSIS / REASONING]
  Now I have the complete diagnosis. The YAML config has `FollowPath` → `RegulatedPurePursuitController`, but the `controller_server` node in Nav2 1.5.1 (Lyrical) has **hardcoded defaults** that load `MPPIController` if the YAML parameter loading mechanism from the launch file isn't matching. The real issue is that the YAML structure uses bare node names like `controller_server:` but the nodes run under `/amr1/controller_server` — so the params don't match.

  The YAML has `controller_server:` as a top-level key. [2026-09-04 13:43:03 UTC [19:13:03 IST]] [STEP 825] [AGENT ANALYSIS / REASONING]
  Now I understand the full problem. The approach of generating per-robot YAML files and passing them as `params_file` to `nav2_bringup.launch.py` **doesn't work** because the file structure has top-level keys like `controller_server:` — but when the node runs under namespace `/amr1`, ROS 2 parameter loading needs the key to be either `/**/controller_server:` or the node needs to be told to look at just `controller_server:`.
  
  The cleanest fix: **drop the separate per-robot YAML files entirely**. Instead, directly embed the frame namespacing into `nav2_bringup.launch.py` by using parameter overrides in the Node declarations, and keep using the base `nav2_params.yaml` as-is (which works when nodes match by bare name). The key insight from the manual `ros2 run` test is that `--params-file` works with the bare-name YAML — so the issue is specifically in how `launch` forwards parameters.
  
  Actually, wait — let me re-read the `ros2 run` test. When I ran manually with `--params-file /tmp/nav2_params_amr1.yaml -r __ns:=/amr1`, it **worked**. So the bare-name YAML does work with namespaced nodes. The problem must be that the launch file is **not actually forwarding the params_file argument**. The LogInfo proved this — it showed the default path, not `/tmp/nav2_params_amr1.yaml`.
  
  The LogInfo showed: `=== NAV2 PARAMS FILE IS: /home/prathmesh/amr_ws/install/.../nav2_params.yaml ===` — i.e., the default, not the `/tmp` override. This means the `launch_arguments` passing from `multi_robot_warehouse.launch.py` → `nav2_bringup.launch.py` is broken.
  
  The `params_file` value in `launch_arguments` is a Python string (`/tmp/nav2_params_amr1.yaml`) being passed to a `DeclareLaunchArgument` that has a default. But the `LogInfo` showed the default was used. This is a known ROS 2 launch scoping issue — `IncludeLaunchDescription` with `launch_arguments` should override the child's declared defaults. Let me verify the install copy actually has the latest code:

[2026-09-04 13:45:58 UTC [19:15:58 IST]] [STEP 855] [AGENT ANALYSIS / REASONING]
  The costmap is looking for `base_link` instead of `amr1/base_footprint`. The problem is that my OpaqueFunction approach for costmap params uses a flat dict with dotted keys like `{'local_costmap.local_costmap.robot_base_frame': 'amr1/base_footprint'}` — but that's not how ROS 2 parameter namespacing works for composed nodes. The costmap is a sub-node of the controller server.
  
  Let me take a simpler approach — just pass the base YAML file but also pass the critical costmap parameters as overrides using the proper nested dict structure:

[2026-09-04 13:48:16 UTC [19:18:16 IST]] [STEP 882] [AGENT ANALYSIS / REASONING]
  The launch-generated temp file uses the **fully-qualified node name** `/amr1/controller_server`. So the parameter matching system works with FQN keys. But our YAML uses `controller_server:` — no namespace prefix. This means the params from our YAML don't match the node's FQN and fall back to the Nav2 1.5.1 hardcoded defaults (MPPI).
  
  But wait — when I ran manually with `ros2 run` and `--params-file /tmp/nav2_params_amr1.yaml -r __ns:=/amr1`, it DID work. That's because `ros2 run` processes the params file differently from `ros2 launch`.
  
  The fix: the generated YAML needs to use the FQN. Let me update the YAML generation in `nav2_bringup.launch.py` to prefix node names with the namespace:

  I need to use the `/**:` wildcard key instead of bare node names, OR use fully-qualified names with the namespace. The `/**:` key is the standard way:

[2026-09-04 13:48:26 UTC [19:18:26 IST]] [STEP 884] [TOOL INVOCATION: replace_file_content]
  Target File:    /home/prathmesh/amr_ws/src/amr_simulation/launch/nav2_bringup.launch.py
  Instruction:    Change the combined dict to use '/**' wildcard or FQN keys for proper namespaced parameter matching
  Description:    Use '/**' wildcard key in generated YAML so params match any node regardless of namespace prefix. This is the ROS 2 standard way to apply params to namespaced nodes.
  Line Range:     80 to 94
  Replacement Snippet:
        # Write a combined, namespaced YAML to /tmp for each robot.
        # Use '/**' wildcard so params match nodes under any namespace.
        # Also include node-specific keys to override properly.

  **All 3 controllers are now `RegulatedPurePursuitController`!** The MPPI bug is **fixed**.
  
  The remaining issue is the TF tree: `Could not find a connection between 'amr1/odom' and 'amr1/base_footprint'`. This means the `odom → base_footprint` transform (published by the Gazebo DiffDrive plugin) isn't connecting to the `map → odom` chain. The static transform publishes `map → amr1/odom`, and the DiffDrive plugin publishes `amr1/odom → amr1/base_footprint` — but these aren't reaching the nav2 nodes because the TF bridge needs to forward them.

The Nav2 nodes are looking for transforms on `/tf` (due to the remapping `/tf → tf`, which maps to the namespaced `/amr1/tf`).
